that the anti-terrorism package, with the joint investigation teams set up at europol, was narrowed down to the european arrest warrant, where i expressly congratulate president berlusconi and president verhofstadt for reaching an agreement which finally made it possible to implement the european arrest warrant. the package of measures extends to the freezing of money and other assets of suspected organisations, as this can cut off the lifeline of terrorism and criminal organisations. but there have also been new considerations that indirectly lead to greater security, such as the recent discussions we have had here, in which we are calling for a visa to be kept as a separate document with a clear identity, just like a residence permit. this is an added security, an added value, which we are achieving here through this discussion. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i would also like to take this opportunity to point out what has been less positive. that is usually the working situation in this house. cooperation with the council is lacking. documents are increasingly being submitted late or not at all, leading to phantom debates in parliament and in committees. the council shines in important committee meetings, but also in plenary, often by absence. the example is monday, when we met specifically in the committee on civil liberties and internal affairs to have important debates on the european arrest warrant, on cooperation with the united states, and on an exchange of letters between president bush and president prodi. the council was conspicuous by its absence, and there was no written information for discussion. this is unacceptable, to say the least, as far as the situation of cooperation is concerned. if you look at the timetables, you also know that they lead to impossible work situations. we're getting more and more last-minute documents. it can't be. we want to make rapid progress, but it must be accompanied by quality. (applause from the centre)
i would like to thank the commissioner for his reply. i am very grateful to the rapporteur, mr paasilinna, for putting the question to the commission, because it clarifies a few things. i also thank commissioner liikanen for his public statement. i would like to add that those of us who have been dealing with these reports - there have been several, the whole package in the last few weeks - have been under enormous pressure, that a lot of misunderstandings have occurred because we have not always been able to inform each other in time of the changes that have taken place. i would point out that these misunderstandings led the greens to want to include the content of the compromise amendments on my behalf, but i did not sign anything for the greens. but i'm back here now. but given the time constraints, i am in agreement and will not withdraw anything. i have only signed two specific amendments, i have not signed the others. it is obvious because i have expressed myself in this way, and i have been understood in this way. this is not a reproach i want to make to individuals, but just another fact i want to point out, which happens when we are under such time pressure, because we wanted to finally go public with this document towards the end of the year. i don't want to have to work on such a delicate problem again in the future. this is really not okay!
mr president, we are probably addressing the question or the comment to the wrong person, but you represent the whole council here with us. i feel that the question of western sahara, to which i have devoted more than 50% of my political life, is really a mourning. it is clear how the conflict is to be resolved. there was the un peace plan in the early 1990s. it is also clear which european countries have very strong geopolitical interests in not following this un peace plan. i must of course take note of the answer when it says that we must follow what is happening in the un. i believe, however, that in the spirit of a common foreign and security policy, the european union and the member states must take a coordinated approach in line with the un peace plan. events have turned upside down since the visit of king mohammed vi. the western sahara was also visited by the french president, jacques chirac, who, contrary to all international law, said that western sahara was the southern provinces of morocco, as we are discussing in the council of the european union.
mr president, mr neyts, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your work. in some of the countries which are preparing to join the union, corruption is a major issue. we are currently negotiating accession with these countries. the funds from privatisation have often disappeared from these countries in dubious ways. do you think it is possible to clarify these points in the context of the accession negotiations or at least to apply clear provisions in this area?
the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. i would like to begin by thanking the rapporteurs, especially mrs van den burg, but also mrs kauppi, very warmly for their cooperation on this report and also for their cooperation with me on the report on crossborder occupational retirement provision. the report on taxation today is a supplement and a continuation of the report for which the codecision procedure was already completed in july of this year. we all agree that we must achieve greater tax coordination on the question of occupational retirement provision, but not just on this one. we are proposing the downstream taxation, the iet system, because it is already a reality in the vast majority of member states. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. in amendment no 1 mrs kauppi has urged the council to make rapid progress on the proposal for a directive on the activities of occupational pension schemes, which will ensure the mutual recognition of occupational pension schemes and will make a significant contribution to actions for effective tax coordination. it is sad that this amendment should be tabled because the ecofin council, like the european council in lisbon, feira, stockholm and wherever it meets, sets priorities and has not yet gone beyond sending a questionnaire. i therefore ask the council: what is the reason why it stresses the urgency but fails to act on it? what are the problems in the council with the commission proposal from october last year? what is the council's position on the van den burg report and the european parliament's decision of july? what are the specific requirements which the council is having problems with? we want answers because we want to continue negotiations so that we can finally implement the objectives of the financial services action plan and the demands of the european parliament for the benefit of citizens and businesses in the sense of the common objectives that have been expressed so often.
- mr president, i have to go. this is going to happen faster than anyone thought. i would first like to apologize to mrs villiers. i am sure that the commission will be able to give us a clear answer to this question, and i hope that the commission will be able to give us a clear answer to this question. a lot has been said. i would just like to add a few things. firstly, the speakers on this and the previous agenda have a great goal. we will do our utmost to ensure that the financial services action plan is adopted and implemented in a timely manner, with high quality and on schedule. this directive is now part of the action plan for financial services and we hope that, unlike in other cases, the council will follow up the commission's and parliament's preparatory work on this issue as quickly as possible by adopting its own position. my predecessor, mr lehne, has already pointed out that the first milestone in fiscal policy at european level was set by the 1998 directive on the effectiveness of accounts. this is the only european legislation to date which has regulated crossborder collateral in connection with financial transactions. we need a uniform regulation of this matter to create an efficient internal market. we need to remove the uncertainties about the law applicable to the crossborder transfer of securities in securities transactions. we need to eliminate the increased administrative burden that stands in the way of a low-cost and integrated eu market. we need to eliminate the legal uncertainty of 15 different legal systems because these different legal systems create a risk in using collateral abroad. we also need to eliminate the disparity in the rules on insolvency. there is therefore a need for a uniform minimum regulation. the directive is the only means of achieving these objectives. we therefore welcome and support the report by the rapporteur and that by mr lehne of the committee on legal affairs and the internal market. thank you very much. we hope that this parliament's report and the commission's proposals will soon become a reality and can be implemented.
- madam president! - what is it? i would like to note that yesterday's minutes show that dr ganzert - the lawyer who was detained in nicaragua - has now been allowed to leave, and i thank you for doing everything you could to make this possible.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your reply. an international convention on the protection of victims of international armed conflicts states: "it is prohibited to use weapons, munitions, and materials and methods of warfare that are likely to cause unnecessary injury or suffering". so much for the theory. the practice is different. now, unfortunately, there are malevolent people in this world who are unscrupulous in their decision to use violence, and who are unscrupulous in their decision to use weapons. but there are also those who take up arms for good reasons - to limit and prevent violence and terror - and who must do so for reasons that are in full accord with human rights. we are calling on them to take up arms, even for good purposes, but not to use them to cause unnecessary injury or suffering, as the text says. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i think we should be very clear about our views on this too. there may be technical possibilities that can be built in here to prevent these injuries and this unnecessary suffering. but at least until then there must be a moratorium, and that is what we are saying today. we also deplore the use of these weapons, not least in afghanistan. but what is more crucial is that we look to the future and that the european union should be very clear and unambiguous in its commitment to such a moratorium in order to prevent, to say it again, unnecessary injury and unnecessary suffering. europe must also make a clear commitment to this. (applause from the centre)
- mr president, i have to go. first of all, let me say that i really enjoy the fact that we can talk in a rather intimate atmosphere here. without a doubt, it is easier to ask important questions than to find the answers. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i would like to thank the commissioner for his reply, but i would also like to thank the rapporteur for his work. we have known since the charter of fundamental rights that the success of such an event depends very much on the presidency. in this context, let me say that i was a little concerned that the presidency of the convention would also be designed to be 'young' and 'dynamic', given the youthful outfit of the president of the council. i was therefore rather pleased to learn, unlike mrs frassoni and mr bonde, that, as is characteristic of high culture, the opinion of the elderly and the wise is still preferred. the installation of the convent was the only outstanding achievement. i regret, from the austrian point of view, paragraph 59, which is very narrow-minded and does not meet the requirements of pan-european safety standards.
- mr president, mr president-in-office of the council, mr commissioner, i would like to thank you for your reply. in its laeken declarations, the european council considers that the european project derives its legitimacy from its democratic, transparent and efficient institutions. if the council then asks the question and also informs the convention of the need to strengthen the role of the european parliament, you will probably not be surprised if i, as a member of parliament, say absolutely yes, because the european parliament is the only institution directly elected by the people. all its meetings are public and transparent - i wish the council were so - and when i see how quickly we are able to take decisions - for example on the european arrest warrant - we are more efficient than the council and in many cases even than the commission. the commission's white paper on european governance and the so-called mandelkern report are therefore welcomed by the laeken council, and i hope that the council will do the same with a view to strengthening european parliamentarism. we would also like to discuss with the commission and the council new forms of governance. the commission has, for example, proposed an interinstitutional working party. i have heard that the council has not accepted this, but has rejected it. i hope that at least now, before barcelona and before seville, there will be intensive discussions with the council and the commission on new forms of governance, which are inconceivable without the consent of the european parliament. i know that some governments are too independent and self-sufficient, and the commission and the council sometimes find it annoying that we 'meddle' too much in details. we are also prepared as the european parliament to accept a new division of labour with the commission and the council, because we too must prepare for an enlarged union. but in our view, any delegation of tasks must be accompanied by a call-back mechanism, because the search for new forms of governance in the form of european governance and the consideration of new allocations of powers between the union and the member states must not come at the expense of european parliamentarism. on the contrary, european parliamentarism must be firmly anchored in the european constitution. let me make one final comment on so-called civil society. the european council has decided to set up a forum and mr barnier, as commissioner, has clearly and often expressed his support. we welcome the establishment of such a forum, but just as such a forum supplements and does not replace the deliberations of the convention, civil society and civil society deliberations do not replace but complement parliamentarism. in this sense, we very much want this dialogue with civil society, with citizens, and we hope that the council and the commission will not see this as contrary to parliamentarism but as complementary to the european parliament. (applause from the centre)
- mr president, i have to go. laeken was better than nice a year ago. i am grateful to the belgian presidency for this. the good result, however, is related to another decision situation which was the case for laeken. the central theme of the weekend was the future of the union. all that was rightly asked were questions. however, a cursory analysis of the questions reveals that some questions are not questions at all, such as the rhetorical question mark whether, in the interests of greater transparency, the meetings of the council should not at least be public in its legislative role. now, of course legislative council meetings must be opened, but why has that not already become the law of the land in maastricht or amsterdam or at the latest in nice? i am thinking of another question in the catalogue of the laeken declarations. the question of whether the charter of fundamental rights should be incorporated into the basic treaty and whether the european community should accede to the european convention on human rights must be considered. does this mean that the results of the last convention are really nothing more than an option? i hope not. i am even more critical of the laeken declaration, which does not even raise any questions. for me, as an elected representative from a medium-sized country, the question arises as to how the convention intends to ensure that the big countries do not become hegemonic in the future. unfortunately, there has been a very specific reason for these questions in recent weeks, and no one should think that in a future europe - soon to be made up of 19 small and mediumsized countries and a small group of large and very large ones - the desire for directorships will diminish. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament. the unimpressive, even unloving way in which the general secretariat of the council handled the convention on fundamental rights last time does not bode well for the work of the constitutional convention. i can only hope that the council bureaucracy will teach us better and serve us very well! (applause from the centre)
- mr president, i have to go. in the laeken presidency conclusions, the word 'entrepreneur' and 'employer' are used only once, in connection with the protection of employees in the event of the insolvency of the employer. i think this is a missed opportunity. we are currently in the process of making the single market a home market at european level, and we have 18 million small and mediumsized enterprises in europe. i am president of the european economic community and it is therefore my concern to stress again and again that one of our main objectives should be to safeguard existing jobs and to create new ones. finally, small and mediumsized enterprises provide two thirds of jobs, pay 80% of taxes and train 85% of young people. the second point, moving away from the internal to the international. how do we envisage the european union being represented in international organisations in the future? we have now decided in parliament to set up this committee for nato. what is the situation with the wto? there are many other areas - how does europe intend to take the lead in the future? the third point that moves me is the council secretariat. i think we should have a panel of the best minds here, including the secretariat. it should be possible for the european council, the european parliament and the commission to be equally represented here. it should be our aim that the best ideas of the best minds will ultimately lead to a result that will bring joy to the citizens of europe.
